Inventory effective settings, logs, backups, document/output, Excel and Monthly Drafts routes, default-token expansion and reparses; no cross-root writes. No app startup or runtime schema migration. Report exact evidence and **STOP**.

**Stage 3 — proposed complete copied-data parity; NOT AUTHORIZED.** On authorized **disposable copies only**, run SQLite `PRAGMA integrity_check`, `foreign_key_check`, `user_version` and a full primary-key-aware all-user-table comparison against the frozen chosen source. If the first PID lingers or GUI cannot be controlled, **STOP; no second launch, forced close or retry**. Distinguish existing-stable preservation from first-time legacy import. **Pass 1 — source/provenance:** distinguished the verified October 20 Z snapshot from potentially newer USB; rejected older recovery/old Z file as fallback; preserved 11 added rows, 22 changed records, 3 pending decisions and Test22 schema compatibility requirement.

**Pass 2 — consistency/rollback:** required closed writers or proven transaction-safe copy, WAL/SHM handling, immutable original, scratch recovery, distinct working target/source, complete all-table parity and exclusion of the operational seed and unsafe output paths.

**Pass 3 — acceptance/scope:** separated copying from settings-file migration and GUI persistence; kept Rob's real-profile backup and normal-path requirements; accounted for failed Codex GUI access and both PID exits; prohibited external sends and any inferred cutover permission. No check is called PASS absent observed stage-specific evidence.

**Every later stage STOP condition:** missing/truncated/conflicting handoff or unexpected branch, uncertain newest source, active writer/unsafe sidecar, hash/integrity/FK/table mismatch, failed restore, accidental operational seed, unapproved path/ACL/profile effects, inability to prevent external integration, or GUI control failure. No retries, silent source substitution, identity changes, ACL weakening, code/launcher/manifest workarounds, process killing, manual data repair or advancement to the next stage without a new explicit Rob instruction.

**Mandatory reporting:** each separately authorized stage must identify exact source, target, allowed reads/writes, branch/commit, dates, hashes, specific PASS/FAIL/NOT RUN/NOT VERIFIED gates, exceptions, real vs reused evidence and smallest next Rob decision. Update BOTH current project `HANDOFF.md` and shared `Shared Notes/Handoff.md`, commit/push on correct branches and **refetch full saved content and verify remote HEAD commit SHA for each**. A documentation update is not application/test execution evidence; never report a stage complete if blocked.

**Current result:** proposal only; **no database was copied/migrated, no CRM was launched, no GUI test/Graph send performed, no existing source or settings modified**. After a successful second launch/check, normally close it and likewise positively verify that second PID exits. The CRM project sets `IsSingleInstance=False`, so a second launch while the first lives could create another process and is specifically **not** an acceptable restart PASS.
7. **Outcome and evidence contract.** Report distinct stages: preparation; GUI access; launch #1; UI Save; close/PID exit #1; launch #2 (if allowed); values reloaded; close/PID exit #2; contained data safety; documentation writeback. **Execute the existing approval in this order, stopping at the first unmet gate:**

Verify that no real-profile/Graph/network/customer action can be reached through application routing; do not treat the Graph-disable flag alone as OS-level isolation. **STOP** if that cannot be demonstrated using the existing controls, rather than adding a new network/ACL framework. Enable `approvedForStart=true` **only in the copied dedicated migration manifest**, invoke its launcher once, positively observe whether a CRM process actually starts, examine contained startup errors/logs, confirm created stable settings and expected semantic email/path/option values, and perform a **normal close**. Do not mark first start PASS merely because PowerShell exits or `Process.Start` returns; capture the application lifetime and result. **Proposed redesign (not implemented).** Use a direct allow ACE for `CodexSandboxOffline` only; do not allow `CodexSandboxUsers` or `CodexSandboxOnline`, and do not add broad `Everyone`, `Users` or `Authenticated Users` rules. Dev must own every object; Dev, Administrators and SYSTEM retain Full Control. Separating source and target directories is the smallest reliable way to let SQLite manage target sidecars without granting directory-level delete/write power over the source. **October 21 evidence reconciled.** The October 21 handoff explicitly recorded that Rob found `settings.json` in the copied Test20 application folder. The old recorded folder `Z:\CRM-T22-M1-Test` is now genuinely not found while `Z:\` itself is accessible; this is not an access-denied result. The later corrected Test20 copy exists at `Z:\CRM Backup 20\CRM-v10-test.20-october14-win-x64`: non-reparse `CRM.exe`, adjacent non-reparse `settings.json` (863 bytes), and `Data\CRM.sqlite` are present. **Resolution logic and reason.** Current normal `OrderFormSettings.Load` resolves `CRM_SETTINGS_PATH`, falling back through `CRMCompatibility.EnvironmentOverride` to `WEEKLY_LOGGER_SETTINGS_PATH`; with neither set, it chooses `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DeltaTrak\CRM\settings.json`. Only if that stable file is absent does it inspect `AppContext.BaseDirectory\settings.json` and import it into the stable path. It does **not** use `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DeltaTrak\WeeklyLogger\settings.json` as a settings source. A side-by-side Test22 stage has a different `AppContext.BaseDirectory` and deliberately contains no settings file, so it cannot automatically discover the adjacent settings in the separate Test20 folder. The real-profile audit policy is stricter: it bypasses normal import and reads only the current Windows profile's stable CRM path, so it correctly fails `STABLE_SETTINGS_MISSING` for `Dev` today.

**Smallest correction / STOP.** Correct the preparation record from an ambiguous “expected settings missing” to: **Dev stable CRM settings has not been created; the applicable Test20 legacy application-adjacent file exists at the corrected Test20 copy; the old Z folder name is stale; the separate WeeklyLogger Local AppData file is not a current-code import source.** For any later separately authorized M1 Prepare evidence, use the operator-confirmed actual Test20 application-adjacent file as the explicit legacy source and the Dev CRM Local AppData path as the stable target. **Static startup finding.** The present candidate cannot safely perform genuine real-profile M1 and is **BLOCKED pending a separately authorized code/launcher change and replacement build**:

1. The VB application framework creates `Form1` in `MyApplication.OnCreateMainForm` (`My Project/Application.Designer.vb:30-32`); `ApplicationEvents.vb` presently has no `Startup` preflight. `Form1.New` initializes controls and calls `OrderFormSettings.Load` (`Form1.vb:74-84`) before `Form1_Load` invokes `Test22IsolationGuard.Validate` (`Form1.vb:102-107`). `OrderFormSettings.LoadFromPaths` can import legacy settings or normalize portable defaults and call `SaveTo` (`OrderFormSettings.vb:43-71`), so the current guard is after a possible real-profile write.
2. `Test22IsolationGuard.Validate` requires contained DB/source/settings/log/GoGreen paths, Graph disabled, contained template/output/agreement/backup settings, nonblank backup, and `.example.test` order recipients (`Test22IsolationGuard.vb:9-41`). Genuine M1 must leave `CRM_SETTINGS_PATH` unset and display the real settings, whose path/email values may legitimately be external. Its containment check uses lexical `Path.GetFullPath` prefix comparison (`Test22IsolationGuard.vb:51-75`) and does not prove protection against a junction/reparse escape.
3. After the late guard, `Form1_Load` calls `CRMDatabase.EnsureReady`, scheduled backup, and repository/data loads (`Form1.vb:102-112`). `EnsureReady` can copy a same-directory legacy DB or configured source into a missing target, create a pre-migration backup, open read/write, select WAL, and apply migrations (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:12-56,112+`; `Data/CRMPaths.vb:14-29`). `RunScheduledBackup` can create/prune files at the loaded real backup path (`Form1.vb:1018-1031`; `Data/CRMBackupService.vb:13-42,84-108`). CRM exception logging honors `CRM_LOG_FOLDER` (`DiagnosticLog.vb:11-30`).
4. The Graph disable check in `GraphAuthService.GetAccessTokenAsync` precedes `EnsureClient`, MSAL construction, and `ProtectedTokenCache` binding (`GraphAuthService.vb:17-24,46-64`), and every identified CRM/Monthly Drafts token route calls that method first (`SettingsForm.vb:73,271`; `MainForm.vb:235`; `OrderProcessingEmailService.vb:24-28`). That alone is not a complete startup boundary: constructing Monthly Drafts eagerly creates `SettingsStore`, `AppLogger`, and `GraphApiClient` (`MainForm.vb:11-19,31-45`); `SettingsStore.New` creates a directory (`SettingsStore.vb:10-28`), `AppLogger.New` always creates/writes under normal LocalAppData and ignores the isolation overrides (`AppLogger.vb:7-38`), and `GraphApiClient.New` constructs an `HttpClient` (`GraphApiClient.vb:63-72`). CRM exposes this constructor through `ShowMonthlyDrafts` (`Form1.vb:1040-1063`; `MonthlyDraftsLauncher.vb:8-12`). Real-profile M1 therefore must block Monthly Drafts before construction, as well as order-email, export, agreement, maintenance, shipping, and settings-save routes.
5. The existing portable launcher/acceptance runner is intentionally synthetic: it creates a disposable settings file and sets `CRM_SETTINGS_PATH` (`Start-Test22-Manual.ps1:63-116`; `RunnerIsolationWorkspace.cs:49-132,149-184`). ### Deliverable and starting point

- **Repository:** `DarkPiglet/Weekly-Logger-Search-Application`.
- **Starting branch:** `codex/crm-contact-save-fix`, exact commit `d502271dd66fa5b0a9491c7ec67e54b51cdbd0c6`. Check the remote ref and local checkout before work. Create a NEW isolated follow-up branch from this exact commit; do not alter the existing source branch. That commit is one ahead of the approved source baseline `3db33ca6ad96ecf72075dc2428069c2d8918ba0c`. The unrelated `codex/application-authentication` / `86a1dff` work must not enter ancestry.
- **Scope:** Fix ONLY the two data-integrity behaviors below, add focused tests, run applicable CRM tests/build, and document verified results. The currently separate Test 22 branch has diverged and contains later work; **do not merge, rebase, cherry-pick Test 22, overwrite Test 22 work, or integrate into `master` in this task**. Integration is a later decision.
- **Expected production-code touchpoints:** `Data/CRMRepository.vb` (`ReplacePrimaryMethod`, and only closely necessary existing call-path code) and `Form1.vb` (`SaveChangesAsync`, `SiteAddressFieldsChanged`, `SiteFieldsChanged`, and directly related preservation logic). Add/extend tests under `tests/CRM.Ui.Tests/` and, only if genuinely necessary, existing CRM data tests; update local and shared handoffs. Do not change schema, UI design, deployment/package tooling, Graph/mail logic, or unrelated functionality. If a wider change is genuinely needed, STOP and explain the conflict instead of silently expanding scope.

### Requirement 1 — existing email/phone method must become primary when explicitly selected

**Observed defect:** The candidate `ReplacePrimaryMethod` returns whenever an active `ContactMethod` row with the same normalized value exists, without checking `IsPrimary`. Editing the displayed primary value to an older saved secondary value can therefore leave the wrong value primary. `PrimaryMethod` (and the CRM display) orders active records by `IsPrimary DESC, ContactMethodId`.

**Required semantics:**
1. If the supplied nonblank value normalizes to the **already active primary** for that contact and method type, perform no unnecessary primary-method updates, inserts, or alterations to stored presentation/history.
2. If it matches an **existing active non-primary** method, promote **that same ContactMethodId** to primary; demote any other active primary **of the same contact and method type** first, in the existing transaction, honoring uniqueness. Do not insert a duplicate, change other method types, or activate archived rows.
3. If it is a genuinely new method, retain the approved existing new-value/normalization behavior; retain preserved earlier method rows/history. For a cleared/blank displayed value, preserve the current approved removal-of-primary behavior (no fabricated new method or unintended deletion).
4. Respect case-normalized email and country-aware phone normalization, but do not rewrite a stored `MethodValue` merely because its formatting differs while the normalized identity is the same. Cover `Email`, `OfficePhone`, and `MobilePhone` as applicable. Avoid new UI or broad normalization redesign.

**Focused acceptance cases:**
- Existing primary chosen unchanged => same primary ContactMethodId, MethodValue, flags, and preexisting rows; no collateral write.
- Existing secondary email chosen via actual `Form1` save path => same existing secondary ContactMethodId becomes **sole active primary**, the old primary remains active/non-primary, and the UI/reload displays the newly selected email.
- At least one office/mobile phone case exercising re-promotion of an existing normalized secondary; validate primary uniqueness per contact/type and all unrelated method types unchanged.
- New value and blank clearing still work; validate no duplicates, inappropriate reactivation, or unintended changes to other contacts.

### Requirement 2 — preserve stored address line 2 in every unrelated or partial address save

**Observed defect:** `Form1.SiteAddressFieldsChanged` compares `original.StreetAddress2` with `""`, and `Form1.SaveChangesAsync` calls `CRMRepository.UpdateSite(..., txtStreetAddress.Text, "", ...)`. `UpdateSite` unconditionally assigns `StreetAddress2=$street2`. This can silently clear a nonblank stored second line even on a contact/site-note save or an intentional edit to ANOTHER address component. The current form does not separately expose an editable `StreetAddress2` control.

**Required semantics:**
1. Treat the existing `StreetAddress2` as **preserved, not user-cleared**, wherever the form has no field for changing it. Do not interpret an empty argument or absent UI control as a request to clear the stored value.
2. Ignore the hidden `StreetAddress2` value when deciding whether the **user edited a visible address component**; preserve it on contact saves, company/site notes, carrier, receiving, salesperson, and other unrelated saves.
3. On an intentional edit to street line 1, city, state, ZIP, or country, **still preserve line 2 unchanged** while updating only intended address components and correctly invalidating `SiteLocation.AddressFingerprint` / geocoding state. Do not suppress a legitimate address invalidation merely to prevent overwriting line 2.
4. Preserve unchanged stored values (including meaningful distinction of NULL/blank where feasible) and existing audit/timestamps for untouched records. Do not add address-line UI, bulk-fix historical records, or modify the database schema.

**Focused acceptance cases:**
- Seed a **disposable** site with a known nonblank `StreetAddress2`; perform contact creation/edit, site-note-only change, and other unrelated site saves. Check line 2 remains identical, site/address fingerprint and location timestamp do not change except where legitimately intended, and no unrelated records/audit rows are created or changed.
- Change a visible address component (e.g., `StreetAddress1`) on the same site: changed component persists, `StreetAddress2` stays identical, fingerprint/geocoding invalidation happens, and the change is audited normally.
- Existing NULL/empty second-line sites and fresh site creation retain previous approved behavior; no phantom "address changed" solely because a hidden nonblank second line exists.
- Test through the real `Form1` save path and verify the final SQLite row after reload/reopen; direct repository-only tests may supplement but must not replace this path.

### Test environment, proof, and stopping conditions

- Use only **independent disposable, writable copies** of test databases for edits and synthetic fixture setup, with test values clearly marked. Never run mutable tests against `Z:\CRM.sqlite`, `Z:\CRM_corrected.sqlite`, the correction manifest, backup storage, or sealed corrected references. A prior corrected reference SHA-256 is `B4B5D575FA9E494605F3BEBEBA9D9B5F85B82867453ED5D6816B03148BCE5B13`; original reference was `FF4EC6C909C38171F5C45258A7147CA4EB79787F8F211D4C0B02C26A97DD7A5D`. Clearly distinguish corrected-copy from seed-only results, avoid tests depending on unapproved network/Office/Graph interactions, and restore any test-only environment variables after each run.

Afterwards verify exact before/after rows and IDs, unrelated rows, activity/audit rows, primary uniqueness, contact/site persistence on reopen, and `PRAGMA integrity_check` and `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`. ## Test 20 Manual Test Findings — October 8, 2026

- The screenshots show the exact Entra result: the app requested read/write access to user/shared mail, send-on-behalf access, basic profile access, and maintained access; Microsoft displayed `Approval required`, then `Request sent`, and the confirmation email records the request as submitted. A Microsoft Entra administrator must review and approve that submitted request. Silent acquisition could not satisfy the newly requested/unapproved permission set and correctly fell back to interactive authentication; no evidence implicates the DPAPI `CurrentUser` cache.
- After the authentication problem, the smoke test attempted to continue but appeared hung for roughly 20 minutes. When Rob attempted to exit, the application told him to allow the test to continue and provided only an OK/wait path, with no user-accessible Cancel/Stop option. Treat this as a usability/recovery defect: a stalled smoke test must not be able to wait indefinitely without a user cancellation path and bounded failure handling.
- Manual use changed the box-order workflow decision. The planned four-way carrier/box-size preselection behavior is cancelled and must be removed/retired, not merely postponed. Do not require Rob to supply the four mapping values. The user should instead open/review the order form, verify the order details, and change the box/order selections there as needed. Do not force or pre-populate a box configuration unless a different workflow is explicitly approved later.

## Recent Decisions

- Standard completion procedure: after every Weekly Logger project task, update this shared handoff with the task result and current project status, including what changed, branch and commit information, files changed, test results, risks, and whether the work is merged or awaiting review. Commit and push the handoff-only update to this shared GitHub repository, then verify the remote commit so ChatGPT can retrieve it without a separate verbal handoff. Follow an explicit contrary instruction when one is given.
- Monthly Drafts email suppression is fulfillment-specific: Boxes sites default to 30 days from the most recent applicable boxes sent (`BoxOrder`) activity date, and Labels sites default to 14 days from the most recent applicable labels sent (`ShippingLabel`) activity date. A Boxes site receiving both in one fulfillment records both activities but remains controlled by the Boxes activity and period. Both settings accept only 0 through 90 days; zero disables the applicable clock. The confirmed per-run override remains non-persistent, and every decision is logged.
- Site names and aliases are limited to 100 trimmed characters in UI, repository validation, and schema triggers. Migration v22 aborts rather than truncating if existing data exceeds that limit.
- New or changed US phone values accept common formatting and extensions, normalize the main number to digits, and require 7 or 10 digits, or 11 beginning with 1. Eight- and nine-digit US numbers are rejected. Non-US values do not use US length rules, duplicate matching uses the same normalization without cross-context US/non-US matches, and unchanged legacy values remain editable.
- Agreement per-unit amounts are capped at $100 in UI and repository validation. The former $1,000,000 UI maximum had no dependent business logic. Payment type `Check` is now `Payment`; migration v22 and the repository compatibility path preserve existing behavior.
- Order-form templates must contain the exact `Order Form` worksheet and merged structural anchors `C8:D8` and `H12:J12` before any output is written. Fields are not auto-mapped.
- Microsoft Graph retains its 60-second timeout. Controlled smoke draft creation may retry once after a timeout not caused by caller cancellation; real batch draft creation never retries automatically.
- Order-processing To/CC recipients are configured in CRM Settings. The optional approval send checkbox is off by default; approval is committed before Graph send, so an email failure does not reverse approval.
- Monthly Drafts has a one-message real-send test requiring an explicitly entered, non-persisted recipient. Graph authentication uses a persistent DPAPI CurrentUser-protected MSAL cache for silent authentication before interactive fallback.
- A contact email is not globally unique. The same normalized email may be used at multiple sites when intentional.
- Reused-email detection is advisory: show the associated sites and allow the save to continue.
- The reused-email advisory is a pre-save Yes/No confirmation, not a validation error. Yes saves all pending changes; No saves nothing and leaves the edits available for correction.
- `DT Associate` changes are confirmed through the normal status bar only and are omitted from modal save summaries.
- The source recycle-call-list workbook is not copied into the application output and is not used for a startup postal-code backfill.
- Agreement records and their site assignments are stored in the CRM database; agreement documents remain managed through the configured document-storage workflow.
- Workbook export paths are configured on the `Order forms` settings tab. Both paths must be set together and must identify separate workbooks with the expected target sheet and headers.
- Manual export replaces only the matching worksheet table rows, preserves workbook formatting and unrelated worksheets, and does not modify the database. Active sites without contacts still receive an RCLog row so site data is not omitted.
- Google geocoding is intentionally on hold while the Google account work is deferred. It is not a release blocker and should not be run unless the user reopens that work.
- The automatic database backup folder is chosen in Settings. CRM checks daily, creates a verified backup when the newest is at least seven days old, and retains the newest 10 managed backups.
- Duplicate candidates are advisory. A merge requires explicit confirmation, creates a backup first, preserves the chosen survivor, moves related associations and history transactionally, fills only blank survivor fields, and archives the duplicate.
- Schema 19 deletion-batch tokens ensure restoration reverses only the selected delete operation and does not revive records that were already inactive.
- CRM is the authoritative Monthly Drafts recipient source. An eligible site is active under an active company and has at least one valid active email; invalid addresses are excluded and sites with no valid address are skipped.
- CRM opens Monthly Drafts as an owned modal window. The standalone Monthly Drafts executable remains supported and resolves `Data\CRM.sqlite` beside the application unless `CRM_DATABASE_PATH` is set.
- Every verified monthly draft records one idempotent CRM `FollowUp` activity dated with the current local date. A verified draft is retained when follow-up recording fails, and the next run reconciles the missing follow-up without creating another draft.
- The Graph Draft ID does not need to appear in the CRM History tab. Future UI work should hide that implementation detail while retaining the identifier in internal audit data and diagnostic logs when it is useful for support or reconciliation.
- The intended next evolution is to rename Monthly Drafts to Monthly Inquiry and send the emails rather than only creating drafts. The user wants a simpler interface because the draft workflow has proven reliable, while keeping detailed operational logging behind the scenes. Sending safeguards and recovery rules still need to be defined before implementation.
- Monthly Inquiry will require a final confirmation before live sending. Its simplified processing view must show sent messages versus total, percent processed, and the current recipient as `Company - City, State/Province, Country`. Graph connectivity and single-recipient test-send controls remain available only under Advanced/Diagnostics.
- Add application-level password protection shared by CRM and standalone Monthly Inquiry; database encryption is out of scope for now. `Robert Nevins` is the initial administrator. Preserve a future-ready user/role/permission model so an administrator can later add users, assign roles, and control function access through a management form.
- An administrator session may open protected Monthly Inquiry functions without another password prompt. A non-administrator must supply an administrator password. Every privileged use must audit the acting user, authorizing administrator, protected function, timestamp, and outcome without storing or logging the password.
- The authenticated user automatically becomes the read-only `DT Associate` for newly added sites and for actual site or contact changes saved for the selected site. Company-only changes do not rewrite all company sites. A successful Monthly Inquiry send also attributes the site to the authenticated user.
- Each eligible site is one Monthly Inquiry message even when it has multiple recipients. A failed message does not stop the batch; processing continues, reports failures, and a later run retries only failed or unsent sites.
- Monthly Inquiry requires separate Pause/Resume and Stop controls. Its progress area also exposes the current monthly-cycle duplicate-prevention state so the user can see which eligible sites were already sent and will not be sent again without an Advanced override.
- First-run authentication will require Robert Nevins to enroll an administrator password and receive a one-time recovery code. Administrator elevation applies to one requested action only, followed by a message explaining what was authorized. Initial administrator permissions cover live Monthly Inquiry sending, same-month resend override, database restore, duplicate merging, future user/role management, Settings, and data export.
- Audit successful and failed login/elevation attempts. In addition to application identities and the protected function, capture the Windows user, computer name, and available local IP addresses. Never store or log the supplied password or recovery code.

## Focused Security Scan Review — October 9, 2026

- The initial high-risk scan review is complete. It covered application/Graph authorization, SQLite reads and writes, file handling, and external inputs. No urgent or critical issue survived deployment-aware review, and no application code, configuration, or dependency was changed.
- **Acknowledged, non-urgent gap:** `Missing application-level authorization boundary`. The app currently relies on the Windows session, the signed-in Entra identity, and Microsoft 365 shared-mailbox permissions rather than app-specific roles. The original finding-one language suggesting broad or tenant-wide Graph access was incorrect and is superseded; do not use it for later risk decisions. Fold this gap and cached-account clarity into the planned schema-v21 roles/authentication work.
- **Finding 2 — low practical exposure under the current single-user testing/deployment model:** `Database\CRM_v10.sql` contains operational contact data, not synthetic placeholders. All 16 unique email addresses in the migration match the current operational seed. The private GitHub repository has retained the migration in history since September 10, 2026 under its prior `WeeklyLogger_v10.sql` name; it was renamed on September 15 and remains in `origin/master` and multiple remote branch histories. All 16 retained `Z:\CRM-v10-test*.zip` packages inspected contain both the v10 migration and `CRM_seed.sqlite`. Rob confirmed that he is currently the only person with access to the Z: location used for these test packages. For off-system testing, he manually copies the ZIP to a personal USB drive kept in his possession, extracts it on that same USB drive, and runs the application from the USB; operational application data is not intentionally copied off the USB except through explicit user-initiated exports. Those RC Log contact and Recycle Agreement Excel exports are company data written to the company's established network directories and traditional business-file locations, where this data was maintained before the CRM existed. The CRM therefore does not make its internal SQLite database the only usable home for the business data; the established exports preserve operational continuity if the application is later retired. Under this current model, treat Finding 2 as a low-priority data-hygiene/hardening issue rather than an urgent vulnerability. The residual risk is principally loss or theft of the unencrypted USB/test media. Do not rewrite Git history or delete retained packages solely for this finding without a separate decision. If deployment expands beyond Rob, reassess this finding before rollout; the intended broader model is a properly installed application on company-controlled systems, not distribution of the portable USB/test package.
- **Former hardening queue:** The four candidates in the original queue have now been validated and dispositioned in the completed scan section below. That completed section supersedes the earlier provisional wording, including the statement that browser argument injection was unverified.
- **Defense-in-depth notes, explicitly not findings:** Graph `@odata.nextLink` origin pinning adds no meaningful capability under the current authenticated Graph/TLS trust boundary; agreement-storage junction/symlink handling adds no demonstrated privilege under the default per-user storage model. Keep these out of the finding count and take no action unless deployment assumptions change.

## Focused Hardening Validation Scan — Completed October 10, 2026

- The read-only focused scan completed against revision `1c7faaa7cda0397607110af2c9f1392688f0c53c` under scan ID `8c93dae8-f9a9-43bf-8dc8-01745e0b3184`. It found two confirmed low-severity issues and two hardening recommendations; no urgent, high, or critical issue was identified. No application source, configuration, dependency, database, package, or project handoff was changed, and `Z:\CRM.sqlite` was not accessed.
- **Candidate 1 — Agreement imports: Hardening recommendation, normal priority; no vulnerability severity assigned.** `AgreementDocumentsForm.vb` exposes `All files`, drag/drop accepts every existing file, import preserves the original extension, and a later explicit Open action passes the managed copy to its registered Windows shell handler. Path basename extraction, generated stored names, managed-root containment, and separate user-initiated import/open actions materially limit the risk. The remaining exposure is operator-assisted social engineering or active content, not automatic execution. Current next state: confirm the genuinely required formats and, during normal hardening work, evaluate one allowlist enforced for both picker and drag/drop plus size, warning, and endpoint-protection behavior; do not change it now.
- **Candidate 2 — Spreadsheet formula handling: one confirmed low-severity finding and two closed paths.** Monthly Drafts `ReportWriter.Csv` quotes fields and escapes quotes but does not neutralize leading spreadsheet formula characters. CRM-derived site names, recipient displays, invalid raw values, and issue messages can therefore reach the local validation CSV; exploitation requires influence over a CRM value and a later user open in formula-evaluating software. The RC Log and Recycle Agreement `.xlsx` paths are **Closed / not reproducible** because both write CRM strings through ClosedXML typed `SetValue` cells rather than formula properties. Current next state: queue CSV neutralization and benign target-application regression tests as low-priority hardening; add future `.xlsx` regression coverage to preserve the closed behavior, but make no changes now.
- **Candidate 3 — Database restore lifecycle: Hardening recommendation, low priority; no confirmed externally exploitable vulnerability.** Restore requires explicit local selection and confirmation and already performs SQLite integrity, foreign-key, and supported-version checks; stages through the SQLite backup API; makes a safety backup; atomically replaces where possible; removes sidecars; revalidates; and has immediate rollback. It does not prove the full expected CRM schema or provenance, and the immediate rollback file is deleted before later migration and repository reload complete. A plausible wrong or crafted SQLite file could therefore cause an outage or manual recovery after operator-assisted restore. Current next state: during normal backup hardening, use temporary non-authoritative fixtures to test incomplete current-version schema and migration failure, then consider a required-schema manifest and retaining rollback through migration/reload; do not change restore behavior now.
- **Candidate 4 — Browser/process arguments: Confirmed finding, low severity.** `OrderFormSettings.IsWebUrl` accepts a quoted HTTP(S) value after URI parsing, but `Form1.OpenShippingPage` discards the canonical parsed URI and interpolates the original string into a manually quoted `ProcessStartInfo.Arguments` value. A harmless Windows `CommandLineToArgvW` test confirmed that a validator-accepted value can split into the intended `--app` value, an extra Chromium switch, and an additional URL. This is browser-option injection, not shell-command injection; it requires control of a saved carrier URL and a later operator action, while the executable remains constrained to Edge or Chrome. Excel and Explorer path argument injection are **Closed / not reproducible**. Current next state: queue canonical-URI plus `ArgumentList` hardening and a regression test in normal work; do not fix it now.
- **Current security next state:** The focused scan review is complete and no additional scan or remediation is authorized. Keep the two confirmed low-severity items and the two recommendations in the normal hardening backlog. The separate operational-data/package verification is already dispositioned above. The missing application-level authorization boundary remains acknowledged and tied to planned roles/authentication work. Graph `@odata.nextLink` origin pinning and agreement-storage junction/symlink handling remain defense-in-depth notes, explicitly not findings. Stop at this review boundary until Rob authorizes implementation or another security task.

## CRM Follow-up Findings — October 12, 2026

- Company data correction exposed a likely soft-delete/name-reuse defect. Rob identified that sites associated with `United States Cold Storage` actually belong to `USA Mex Cold Solutions`. After soft-deleting the existing `USA Mex Cold Solutions` company, he attempted to edit/rename the remaining company record to `USA Mex Cold Solutions` and change its customer number. Save failed with: `Failed to save changes. SQLite Error 19: UNIQUE constraint failed: Company.CompanyName.`
- Working hypothesis only: the soft-deleted USA Mex Cold Solutions row may still reserve the company name under the database-wide unique constraint. Codex must verify the actual schema/repository behavior before changing code or data; do not assume the hypothesis is the root cause.
- Required regression coverage: test soft-deleting a company and then creating a new active company with the same name; test soft-deleting a company and renaming a different active company to the deleted company's name; verify expected behavior for customer-number changes in the same edit; verify archived/history integrity is preserved and no unintended record is reactivated or overwritten. Include the ordinary remove-company/add-different-company path as a control because that workflow has worked previously.
- October 12 recovery result: the hypothesis was confirmed. The table-wide `Company.CompanyName` constraint included archived rows, and company/site create upserts could reactivate and overwrite archived records. Migration v24 and repository changes now enforce names only among active records and preserve archived rows. The required regression matrix passes; manual confirmation remains pending in the October 12 package.
- Box-order workflow is confirmed: do not restore CRM Small/Large box-size selection or the former FedEx/UPS size mappings. The CRM should open/populate the Excel order form only with reliable known data; the user enters the actual box/order item selections and quantities directly in the Excel form.
- Territory/salesperson modeling is explicitly deferred. Do not change company/site territory or salesperson behavior in this work package. Preserve the design question for later review: company billing territory/salesperson versus site shipping territory, with no new site-salesperson relationship until an authoritative business rule/source is confirmed.

### Company/site duplicate and correction business rules

- Soft-deleted company and site records preserve history and audit data but must not permanently block reuse of a company or site name. Exact duplicate names among active records should still be prevented.
- Because incoming customer paperwork may identify the same real organization or location inconsistently, CRM should warn about potential duplicates using available evidence such as company name, customer number, site name, address, phone, and email. Similarity is advisory only: never automatically merge, overwrite, reactivate, or reassign records without explicit user confirmation.
- A matching physical address is not proof of a duplicate and must never by itself block a save or trigger a merge. One physical location may legitimately return for multiple companies. For example, separate site records such as `Kroger - Modesto, California` and `Publix - Modesto, California` may share the same street address because that location is returning on behalf of multiple companies, and it may also return for itself.
- When CRM flags a possible duplicate, show the matching context so the user can distinguish a true duplicate from a legitimate multi-company/shared-location relationship or genuinely separate records.
- Confirmed duplicates should use the existing user-confirmed merge workflow, preserving related sites, contacts, agreements, activities, revision/audit history, and other associations.
- A surviving active record must be correctable, including renaming it to the name of a previously soft-deleted record, without a database UNIQUE-constraint failure solely because the archived row remains present.
- Regression testing must cover normal company/site creation, soft deletion, active-name reuse, rename-after-deletion, duplicate warnings, legitimate same-address/different-company sites, and confirmed merge behavior, while verifying that history is preserved and no unrelated record is reactivated, overwritten, or reassigned.


## CRM Testing Finding — October 13, 2026

A follow-up synchronization defect was discovered: after saving the company rename, the associated site names retained the old company-name prefix instead of following the renamed company.

## CRM database recovery read-only investigation findings (October 19, 2026)

### Scope, inputs, and stopping condition

- This was an investigation only. No recovery, merge, application execution, application/source/schema/settings change, production access, live email, test run, package, or deployment was performed. The original installations were not opened. The only intended repository modification is this sanitized handoff report.
- The mounted inputs were verified at `Z:\CRM_Recovery\Backup\Data\CRM.sqlite` and `Z:\CRM_Recovery\Everyday\Data\CRM.sqlite`. Each main file is 2,195,456 bytes. Backup SHA-256 is `5A39A8E77372758DE417392A45B162BB94AD4DAC28721837578913F10E9C3EE6`; Everyday SHA-256 is `308F7718F5FD8D3B4D5AA64B2AB02B5D3F374D8D3DE3787A2576397B90C54140`. The distinct hashes confirm distinct database states.
- At initial enumeration, each copied Data folder contained only `CRM.sqlite`; no WAL, SHM, rollback journal, settings file, or source-connection configuration was present. Consequently the mounted database paths are verified, but the original installations' connection settings cannot be independently verified from these Data-only copies.
- Main-file copied creation times were `2026-10-20T02:33:05.3557769Z` (Backup) and `2026-10-20T02:34:50.9115725Z` (Everyday). Preserved main-file last-write times were `2026-10-19T16:04:56Z` and `2026-10-16T19:57:04Z`, respectively. Backup's latest internal write evidence ends at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1783863Z`; Everyday's ends at `2026-10-16T19:56:12.6310588Z`. These facts are consistent with Rob's statement that work stopped after discovery, but timestamps alone cannot prove that no unlogged or timestamp-preserving access occurred.
- **Mandatory stop / access-method incident:** Microsoft.Data.Sqlite connections were created with `SqliteOpenMode.ReadOnly`, private cache, pooling disabled, and `PRAGMA query_only=ON` (verified as `1`). Both databases report persistent `journal_mode=wal`. Despite the read-only/query-only connection, SQLite created a 32,768-byte `CRM.sqlite-shm` and a zero-byte `CRM.sqlite-wal` in each copied folder during inspection. Backup sidecars were created at `2026-10-20T03:07:42.5476605Z` (SHM) and `2026-10-20T02:59:38.9473094Z` (WAL); Everyday sidecars at `2026-10-20T03:07:42.9656662Z` and `2026-10-20T02:59:59.1335391Z`. Both SHM files hash to `FD4C9FDA9CD3F9AE7C962B0DDF37232294D55580E1AA165AA06129B8549389EB`; both empty WAL files have the standard empty SHA-256 `E3B0C44298FC1C149AFBF4C8996FB92427AE41E4649B934CA495991B7852B855`. The two main database hashes remained unchanged after every query. No further SQLite access was performed once the sidecars were discovered, and the sidecars were not deleted or altered. This means the investigation is evidence-rich but stopped short of any additional checks because companion-file writes could no longer be excluded.

### Read-only method and validation completed before the stop

- Filesystem identity was checked with PowerShell `Get-Item`, `Get-ChildItem`, and `Get-FileHash`. SQLite metadata and rows were read with the repository's Microsoft.Data.Sqlite runtime using explicit read-only connections and `PRAGMA query_only=ON`; comparisons were performed in process between two separately read-only connections. An attempted URI `ATTACH ... mode=ro` failed with SQLite error 14 and attached nothing; main-file hashes remained unchanged. No SQL write statement was issued.
- Both main files returned `PRAGMA integrity_check = ok`, zero `foreign_key_check` rows, `PRAGMA user_version=24`, and `SchemaVersion=24`. Both use 4,096-byte pages and 536 pages. Backup had 12 free pages; Everyday had 14. Their table definitions and schema objects matched; no schema migration is involved.
- The approved source behavior was checked without checkout or modification against remote branch `codex/test22` at `97ed38f311a26f8bd5282cebf13b73d623629982`. The working checkout's isolated unauthorized authentication branch was not run or used as the recovery baseline. Source inspection confirms that `Activity` is the principal record-history/audit surface, normal site/contact saves can update timestamps even when semantic values do not change, contact methods have no timestamps, and a site deletion records one `SiteRemoved` event while soft-deleting related links and otherwise-unshared contacts.
- Whole-table, primary-key-aware comparison found differences only in `Activity`, `Company`, `Site`, `Contact`, `ContactMethod`, `SiteContact`, `RecycleProgramProfile`, and `SiteLocation`. Agreements, agreement items/sites/documents, accounts, aliases, sales/source mappings, imports/issues/rows, items, monthly follow-ups, security tables, associates, users/roles/permissions, and vendor accounts were unchanged. `SecurityAuditEvent` and `SecurityAuditNetworkAddress` are empty in both copies; there is no separate general row-version table.

### Observed October 19 Backup work

- Backup contains 16 history rows created from `2026-10-19T14:23:04.7846715Z` through `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1783863Z`: five `SiteUpdated`, three `ContactAdded`, four `ContactUpdated`, two `BoxOrder`, one `PostersSent`, and one `SiteRemoved`. The Posters event was created October 19 but deliberately records an occurrence date of April 24, 2026; both dates must remain distinct in any future recovery.
- The 16 events affect existing companies C-2 and C-76; existing sites S-3, S-230, S-233, and S-237; existing contacts C-3, C-206, C-220, and C-221; and three new Backup contacts C-507 through C-509. Numeric labels here are sanitized internal IDs, not customer names or personal data.
- Three contacts were added: C-507 at S-237 and C-508/C-509 at S-230. Together they have six new contact-method rows and three new active site-contact links. Each new contact has exactly one site link and two active methods. C-507 also has a subsequent same-day Office phone and Notes update.
- Cleanly evidenced existing-record edits are: S-3 Notes cleared; C-220 and C-221 Notes changed; S-230 Notes changed twice and ended blank; S-237 Notes changed twice and ended at a different value from Everyday; two BoxOrder activities at S-237 linked to C-221 and C-220; `LastInteractionDate` at those two links advanced to October 19; and one backdated PostersSent activity at S-230. Audit old-value fingerprints for S-3, S-230, C-220, and C-221 match Everyday's current values, so these edits begin from the authoritative current value rather than an older divergent value.
- S-233 was soft-deleted. One archive-batch token ties S-233, its two site-contact links, and the two otherwise-unshared contacts C-210/C-211. Backup has those five rows inactive; Everyday has them active. The single `SiteRemoved` history event is company-linked but has null `SiteId` and `ContactId`; its details identify the site, and the batch token/timestamps corroborate S-233, but the foreign-key linkage is incomplete. No separate ContactRemoved events were created for the cascade.
- Backup writes also touched two Company timestamps, four Site timestamps, 11 Contact timestamps, three program-profile timestamps, and three location timestamps. Most of that is timestamp-only save churn and must not overwrite Everyday timestamps. Four existing contact-method rows differ only because Backup marks the sole active email method primary where Everyday marks it non-primary; contact methods have no timestamps, and only one related user-visible phone change has an audit event, so attribution of all four primary-flag differences to October 19 is an inference rather than a proven fact.

### Everyday changes, collisions, and conflicts

- Activity rows 1 through 1346 are identical in both databases. Everyday then added four October 16 events at IDs 1347-1350 (two S-237 updates, one S-237/C-220 BoxOrder, and one unrelated shipping-label event). Backup independently reused IDs 1347-1350 on October 19 and added IDs 1351-1362. No future recovery may preserve or copy any Backup `ActivityId`; all 16 must receive new IDs with an explicit old-to-new map.

Source review explains that clearing a primary method only removes its primary flag and does not deactivate the method, after which the read path can still return it. ### S-237 Notes — first-priority conflict

- **Current authoritative Everyday value:** SQL `NULL` (no Notes text), with profile `UpdatedUtc=2026-10-16T19:32:45.6458802+00:00`. **Current Backup value:** `Windigo Logistics sending in for Kroger.`, with profile `UpdatedUtc=2026-10-19T15:12:43.7915307+00:00`.

- Backup Activity 1348 at `2026-10-19T14:35:33.5300145+00:00` records the same newline normalization from its stale pre-October-16 value; Activity 1350 at `2026-10-19T14:35:56.7608122+00:00` records that full value changing to `Windigo Logistics sending in for Kroger.` Thus, relative to the older Backup value, October 19 replaced the multi-part note with one retained sentence and removed `box order` plus the sentence beginning `Ben is trying`; relative to current Everyday, it adds the retained sentence to an empty field.
- The later Backup profile timestamp does not prove an additional Notes edit because no later Notes audit exists. The two branches contain genuinely divergent user actions; neither timestamp alone resolves intent.
- **Resolution options, none implemented:** (A) retain Everyday `NULL` and preserve the October 19 sentence only in recreated audit/history; (B) set Notes to the Backup sentence while preserving both October 16 clearing history and October 19 history; (C) place the Backup sentence in a separately chosen current note/history location while leaving this field empty. - **C-206 MobilePhone:** Everyday and Backup both currently contain one active MobilePhone method with the same email-formatted value; it is not a conventional phone number. ### Confirmed findings

- Reliable site evidence identifies Blue Ash as active S-230 under C-76. Everyday S-230 has three active pre-existing contact links, C-205/C-206/C-207. Backup retains all three links unchanged as active and adds active links to C-508 and C-509; no existing Blue Ash contact was displaced.

Within each pair, normalized full name, JobTitle, Notes, Email value/normalization/validity, and OfficePhone value/normalization/validity match exactly. A whole-contact exact-name search found only each original and its corresponding new record. The new method rows use new IDs but reproduce both old method values exactly.
- The database represents the operation as recreation, not reassociation: Backup Activity 1358 records C-508 `ContactAdded` to S-230 at `2026-10-19T15:58:04.4570978+00:00`; Activity 1359 records C-509 `ContactAdded` at `2026-10-19T15:58:04.4695232+00:00`. There is no S-230 link for C-210 or C-211 in either snapshot and no ContactId-linked Activity row for those original IDs.

All four recreated method rows have `IsPrimary=1`, whereas the matching original method rows have `0`. The new records also have October 19 creation timestamps rather than the originals' August 23 timestamps. C-509's S-230 link has `LastInteractionDate=2026-10-02`; the other old/new pair links have no LastInteractionDate.
- S-233 was deleted about 63 seconds after the two recreated contacts were added. S-233 became inactive at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.0822800+00:00`; C-210/C-211 became inactive at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1775984+00:00`; Activity 1362 records SiteRemoved at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1783814+00:00`. One archive token marks S-233, its C-210/C-211 links, and the two original contacts. The active S-230 links and C-508/C-509 have no archive token.
- Therefore the deletion cascade did not deactivate the Blue Ash contacts or links. It deactivated C-210/C-211 because those original IDs still had only their S-233 links. Rob's active-use intent survived in Backup through C-508/C-509, but identity/history is split across duplicate IDs. Restoring S-233 as-is would revive C-210/C-211 and create two active duplicate pairs alongside C-508/C-509.

- **Recommended identity-preserving option:** treat C-508→C-210 and C-509→C-211 as recovery identity maps, retain original C-210/C-211 as the survivor records, add active S-230 links for those survivors, retain existing S-230 links C-205/C-206/C-207, and soft-delete only S-233 plus its S-233 links. ## Read-only dry-run failure investigation result — October 19, 2026

**Status: root cause confirmed from existing text source; no code or database access/execution.**

### Evidence inventory and limitation

- Existing harness source was available read-only at `C:\Users\Dev\.codex\visualizations\2026\10\20\01a15739-b7ba-70c1-be28-927112e34e5a\crm_recovery_dryrun.py` (30,391 bytes; SHA-256 `4EF3815C75347D6017A51EF12E562F1CB52812A6834D02E09B493AD6103E76F8`). The complete 655-line file was reviewed as text only and was not edited or executed.
- The reconciled public manifest, conflict decisions, timestamp provenance, and prior sanitized failure report are present in this handoff. No standalone execution log or stack traceback file exists in the harness directory. The prior captured output supplies only `IndexError: tuple index out of range`, timestamps, mappings, preflight data, clone path, and `rollback=PASS`; it contains no traceback frame or source line. Harness lines 627–641 explain that limitation: the exception handler serializes only `type(exc).__name__` and `str(exc)`, not a traceback. No SQLite file was opened, copied, inventoried, or searched to fill the gap.

### Confirmed root cause and exact operation

- The defect is in harness control logic, not the recovery manifest. `expected_rows()` lines 296–368 constructs keys through `key_for()` lines 145–146. A ContactMethod key is a one-element tuple `(ContactMethodId,)`; a SiteContact key is a two-element tuple `(SiteId, ContactId)`.
- Main lines 543–544 iterate the two new ContactMethod keys with loop variable `key`. With the deterministic map, the final value left in that variable is `(1059,)`, arity 1, with valid position `[0]` only.
- Main lines 545–546 then begin selecting new SiteContact rows with generator variable `k`. The intended candidate is `k=(SiteId, ContactId)`, arity 2, so the filter correctly reads `k[0]` for SiteId but incorrectly reads stale outer variable `key[1]` for ContactId. Because `key` is still `(1059,)`, position `[1]` does not exist and Python raises exactly `IndexError: tuple index out of range`. The tuple producer is `key_for()`; the faulty consumer is line 546. The smallest correction would replace both `key[1]` references on line 546 with `k[1]`. This is a proposal only; the harness was not changed.
- Source order establishes the last completed boundary more precisely than the earlier sanitized report: the C-507 Contact insert at line 542 and both ContactMethod inserts at lines 543–544 completed within the open transaction; evaluation failed in the SiteContact generator before any SiteContact insert at line 548. None of the profile/contact/link/archive updates, 16 Activity inserts, precommit checks, commit, or postcommit checks at lines 550–623 ran. All three completed inserts were inside the transaction that successfully rolled back, and the retained clone main hash matching E proves no durable recovery state. This does not make any partial manifest operation a pass.

### Impact assessment

- Deterministic mappings were not changed or contradicted: C-507→C-507, methods 1058/1059→1058/1059, C-508/C-509→C-210/C-211, and Activities 1347–1362→1351–1366 remain the reviewed plan. The exception resulted from using the wrong Python variable while filtering already constructed SiteContact keys; it did not arise from tuple contents supplied by the manifest.
- No Activity row was inserted, so none of the 16 historical Activity timestamps or relationship mappings—including the sole Activity 1362 `SiteId=S-233` repair—was exercised. Their preflight source evidence remains as previously documented, but result correctness is unverified.
- Rollback is evidenced by `rollback=PASS` plus the failed clone's byte-identical E main hash and absence of WAL/SHM after close. That establishes no durable recovery, not successful application. All mandatory post-write precommit checks and every postcommit check remain `NOT RUN`.

### Proposed smallest correction and future validation — not authorized now

- Proposed code edit: on existing harness line 546 only, change `(key[1] == plan["new_contact"] or key[1] in (210, 211))` to `(k[1] == plan["new_contact"] or k[1] in (210, 211))`. Do not alter approved IDs, conflict decisions, timestamps, or manifest content to accommodate the defect.
- Before any future clone attempt, a separate authorization should permit non-CRM synthetic harness checks that assert SiteContact keys have arity 2 and that the filter selects exactly `(237,N507)`, `(230,210)`, and `(230,211)` while excluding update-only/archive keys. It should also permit a focused fault-injection check of the transaction wrapper on a toy in-memory database so rollback and step-level diagnostic capture are proven without touching CRM evidence.
- A separately authorized real attempt should add a sanitized `last_completed_step` and traceback line capture, reverify all source/evidence identities, mappings, protected hashes, expected counts, and historical timestamps before writes, then require the existing single-transaction assertions. Before commit it must validate exact rows and primary-key-aware diffs, all five Blue Ash links, archive state, all 16 Activity mappings and original timestamps, integrity, and foreign keys. After commit it must independently reopen and repeat integrity, foreign-key, exact-diff, mapping, count, and timestamp checks. Any failure must roll back or quarantine as applicable and must not be retried ad hoc.

The prior authorization remains exhausted. Editing the harness, running synthetic checks, executing another clone attempt, accessing any SQLite file, or performing recovery requires Rob's separate explicit authorization.

Relevant counts: Associate 20, CompanySalesAssignment 144, Site 426, Activity 1,373, Company 144, Contact 508, SiteContact 517.

### Schema, migration, and application finding

- The original normalized design combined two business roles in one `Associate` table. Import specification and `RcLogImporter` both resolve `Sales Person` and `Associate who made contact` through the same `GetOrCreateAssociate` routine. `Associate` has only `AssociateId`, case-insensitive unique `DisplayName`, and `IsActive`; it has no DT-associate/salesperson role discriminator.
- `CompanySalesAssignment.AssociateId` and `Activity.AssociateId` both reference that mixed table. Other Associate foreign keys are `AppUser.AssociateId`, `AgreementItemRate.DeletedByAssociateId`, and `AgreementDocument.DeletedByAssociateId`.
- Migration 7 added company salesperson/territory text. Migration 8 copied ownership to free-text `Site.SalesPerson`/`Site.TerritoryNumber`. Migration 18 added free-text `Site.DTAssociate` and backfilled it from the latest qualifying Activity's Associate. Migration 21 canonicalized Robert/Robet to Robert Nevins and linked AppUser to Associate. Migration 24 did not add role constraints.
- Therefore internal DeltaTrak associates and external salespeople were combined during normalization by design, and later denormalized into two unconstrained Site text fields. The requested rule “DT Associates should only be Robert Nevins, [Chelsie/Chelsea] Bonstein, Kala Pendergrass” cannot safely be enforced by deleting every other Associate row; those other rows carry salesperson assignments.
- Current site screens read/write free-text `Site.SalesPerson`; they do not use `CompanySalesAssignment` as their current-owner source. Site saves accept arbitrary salesperson spelling/case. Qualifying Activity insertions copy `Associate.DisplayName` into `Site.DTAssociate`. The current unapproved authentication branch also sets a site's DT associate from the signed-in AppUser Associate; because AppUser has no DT-role constraint, a future user linked to a salesperson row could recreate a mixed-role value. The legacy RC Log importer also accepts raw names and can recreate spelling variants if reused. These are recurrence risks, not authorization to change code.

### Group 1 — the three DT-associate identities

- Associate 2 `Robert Nevins`: already canonical. Related: 718 Activities, AppUser 1, one AgreementDocument deletion reference; no sales assignments.
- Associate 4 `Chelsie`: intended full name is the same person referred to by the request as Chelsea/Chelsea Bonstein, but the attachment explicitly spells the target `Chelsie Bonstein`. Related: 114 Activities; no sales assignments. Canonical spelling requires confirmation before any write.
- Associate 7 `Kala`: intended `Kala Pendergrass`. Related Activities 39 and 40, both at Site 17; no sales assignments.
- The other 17 Associate rows are not evidence of 17 additional DT associates. They include salespeople and operational identities and must not be blanket-deleted.

### Application recurrence risks and recommendations — no code changes made

- `Site.SalesPerson` and `Site.DTAssociate` are unconstrained text. Site save paths can reintroduce spelling/case variants. Later work should replace free text with canonical IDs or, at minimum, controlled role-filtered selectors and centralized alias normalization.
- `Associate` still mixes internal DT associates, outside salespeople, and operational identities with no role discriminator. Add an explicit role model or separate identity/assignment concepts before enforcing “three DT associates.”
- `RcLogImporter.GetOrCreateAssociate` creates both salespeople and activity associates from raw names. Future code should resolve approved aliases to canonical identities and reject or review unknown variants rather than insert them directly.
- Qualifying Activity creation copies the generic Associate display name into `Site.DTAssociate`. Enforce a DT-associate role at that boundary. The isolated, unapproved authentication branch also links AppUser to the same unrestricted Associate table; if that work is ever reconsidered, require the same role constraint.
- Current UI reads `Site.SalesPerson`, while normalized `CompanySalesAssignment` holds dated assignment history. Add one transactional handoff service that closes the old assignment, opens the replacement, and updates all in-scope Site rows together; prevent ordinary site edits from silently diverging from normalized current ownership.

### Static startup/shutdown findings

- Database resolution uses `CRM_DATABASE_PATH`, then legacy `WEEKLY_LOGGER_DATABASE_PATH`, then `Data\CRM.sqlite` under `AppContext.BaseDirectory`; the seed override follows the same current/legacy precedence (`Data/CRMPaths.vb:8-29`, `Data/CRMCompatibility.vb:14-18`). No command-line database override exists, and the process working directory does not control these defaults.
- `Form1_Load` calls `CRMDatabase.EnsureReady`, immediately runs the scheduled-backup check, constructs the repository, and calls `MigrateAgreementDocuments` before loading UI data (`Form1.vb:102-112`).
- `EnsureReady` creates the target from a legacy/seed database if the target is absent, creates an adjacent migration backup when schema is below 24, then opens the target read-write, applies migrations, and validates it (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:12-42,59-98,100-153`). Every normal connection is `ReadWrite`, enables foreign keys/busy timeout, and executes `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:44-56`). A schema-24 corrected copy avoids migrations/backups, but WAL/header/sidecar writes remain unavoidable.
- Settings resolve through `CRM_SETTINGS_PATH`, then legacy `WEEKLY_LOGGER_SETTINGS_PATH`, then application-adjacent `settings.json` (`OrderFormSettings.vb:26-31`). Loading can normalize paths and save the settings file, and the legacy/default agreement-folder migration can move managed documents and save settings (`OrderFormSettings.vb:34-53,120-152`). A missing settings file defaults agreement documents to live Local AppData.
- Startup `MigrateAgreementDocuments` opens a read-write transaction, prepares/renames company folders and ownership markers, moves files when present, and can update `AgreementDocument.StoredRelativePath` (`Data/CRMRepository.vb:88-198`; `AgreementDocumentStorage.vb:10-55`). It cannot be treated as display-only I/O. Its root must be an isolated local folder and any writes must be authorized only there and in the disposable DB.
- Scheduled backup runs once at startup and then every 24 hours; an empty `BackupFolder` disables creation (`Form1.vb:90,106-110,993-1016`; `Data/CRMBackupService.vb:13-43`). Diagnostic logs default to live Local AppData but support `CRM_LOG_FOLDER`/legacy override and write only on handled/unhandled errors (`DiagnosticLog.vb:11-45`).
- This package has no startup authentication. Graph, browsers, Excel, exports, geocoding, and Monthly Drafts are user-invoked paths, not main-window startup work. Network access should nevertheless be disabled and those commands must remain untouched.
- Normal close stops/disposes the backup timer. If the UI becomes dirty, close offers Save/Discard/Cancel and Save invokes database writes (`Form1.vb:1013-1016,2061-2090`). `SaveMySettingsOnExit=True` is configured, but this commit has no `Settings.settings`; whether the VB runtime creates profile metadata is not proven. A disposable Windows profile/sandbox is therefore required.

### Startup-write risk table

| Path/asset | Trigger and possible write | Required mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Disposable `CRM.sqlite` | `EnsureReady` and every repository connection open read-write and set WAL; migration if below v24 | Use only a fresh local working copy; immutable preflight must prove v24; retain a sealed reference copy; allow DB/WAL/SHM writes only in the disposable root |
| Adjacent migration backup | Target below v24 | Fail closed unless both `user_version` and `SchemaVersion` are 24; reject any created backup as a preflight failure |
| Application `Data\CRM.sqlite` / seed | Used if the override is missing or target disappears | Set current and legacy DB/source variables; remove/replace the extracted default DB with a fail-closed sentinel and deny writes to extracted `Data`; verify target exists immediately before launch |
| `settings.json` | Load-time normalization/migration may save | Precreate an isolated absolute settings file; set current and legacy settings variables; hash it before/after |
| Agreement documents | Startup may create/rename folders/markers, move files, and update DB paths | Point settings to an empty isolated root, preflight DB paths/markers, authorize any resulting writes only there; never point to live Local AppData/network/USB paths |
| Backup folder | Immediate weekly check can create/prune backups | Set `BackupFolder` to blank; verify no inherited settings value |
| Diagnostic logs | Startup exception/unhandled error | Set current and legacy log variables to an isolated log folder |
| User profile/config | Possible VB exit metadata; Local AppData defaults if isolation fails | Use a fresh disposable Windows Sandbox/VM or local test profile with no mapped Z drive and no production profile access |
| Network/Graph/browser | Not automatic in this build, but available through commands | Disable network; do not open Monthly Drafts, shipping, Settings, Maintenance, export, or email paths |

## Existing CRM virtual-machine isolation investigation — October 20, 2026

**Classification: SUITABLE ONLY AFTER SPECIFICALLY IDENTIFIED AND APPROVED CHANGES.** The current Windows instance is already a guest VM; absence of nested Windows Sandbox or Hyper-V tooling inside it is not evidence against using this VM. It is unsuitable *as currently configured* because the guest has direct read/write visibility to the host-provided `Z:` share, can read the preserved CRM production artifacts and guest documents, and has an active routed network interface. No CRM process was launched, no files were copied, and no VM, drive, network, permission, database, code, or application setting was changed.

### Verified guest and virtualization evidence

- Guest firmware/registry identify `SystemManufacturer=QEMU`, product `Ubuntu 24.04 PC (Q35 + ICH9, 2009)`, and an Ubuntu EDK II firmware build. The QEMU Guest Agent, SPICE Agent, VirtIO-FS service, WinFsp launcher, and corresponding VirtIO drivers are installed; QEMU Guest Agent, SPICE Agent, VirtIO-FS, and WinFsp were running. The environment is therefore a QEMU/KVM-style Windows guest, likely managed from an Ubuntu/libvirt host, although the physical host and its exact management configuration cannot be inspected or proven from inside the guest.
- The guest has one QEMU virtual hard disk (`C:`), two QEMU virtual optical devices (`D:` and `E:`), and a host share mounted as `Z:`. `D:` contains a read-only `OFFICE14` UDF medium; `E:` is empty/not ready. No other filesystem drive, SMB mapping, persistent mapped network drive, or `SUBST` drive was observed.
- `Z:` is not SMB. It is labelled `VM-Share`; the active VirtIO-FS service explicitly provides access to host-shared directories. Windows reports the mount as read/write NTFS, and its root ACL includes `Everyone: FullControl`. Write access was not exercised.

### Resources currently accessible from the guest

- The guest can enumerate `Z:` and read `Z:\CRM.sqlite`, `Z:\CRM_corrected.sqlite`, `Z:\CRM_correction_manifest.json`, `Z:\Data for Priya.docx`, CRM packages, test documents, and other host-shared content. This directly proves current access to production databases/documents. The known database files were opened only for a one-byte read test and were not opened read-write or changed.
- No SMB connection or ordinary mapped network drive is active, but this does not restrict VirtIO-FS or prove that unmounted network resources are unreachable.
- The active guest Ethernet interface has DHCP address `192.168.122.24/24`, gateway/DNS `192.168.122.1`, and a default route. Windows Firewall is on but permits outbound traffic. DNS resolution and the authorized GitHub fetch/push path work; a direct TCP probe from the restricted Codex process did not, showing that network access depends on execution context rather than being reliably absent. The libvirt-style subnet suggests NAT, but NAT versus bridge and access to host/LAN services cannot be proven from the guest alone.
- The only connected USB-class devices observed were the virtual xHCI controller and root hub; no USB peripheral or USB storage device was present. The controller means later host-side passthrough/hot-plug remains possible unless the VM configuration prevents it. No such prevention could be verified from the guest.
- The active interactive console belongs to guest user `Dev`; investigation commands run as restricted user `CodexSandboxOffline`. That restricted identity nevertheless has explicit read/execute access to `C:\Users\Dev\Documents` and `C:\Users\Dev\source`, while `Dev` has full control. Thus neither likely launch identity is presently isolated from the live guest profile and repository/documents. An active SPICE Agent is another host-integration channel; no SPICE WebDAV filesystem mount was observed, but host-side clipboard/file-transfer policy is unverified.
- Final preservation recheck: `Z:\CRM.sqlite` remained `FF4EC6C909C38171F5C45258A7147CA4EB79787F8F211D4C0B02C26A97DD7A5D`; `Z:\CRM_corrected.sqlite` remained `B4B5D575FA9E494605F3BEBEBA9D9B5F85B82867453ED5D6816B03148BCE5B13`; and `Z:\CRM_correction_manifest.json` remained `8AA91C24DD1F70CF6DC737A94508BB811DDECDFAC8B2EEA942B9BFB6D3992E24`. Their recorded timestamps were unchanged, and no CRM process was running.

### Required controls and uncertainties

- The reliable control for `Z:` is a host-hypervisor change: power off the guest and detach the `VM-Share` VirtIO-FS device (or use a clone without it), then boot and verify that `Z:` and all known production paths are absent. Stopping the guest service or relying on path environment variables is weaker and does not prove the host share cannot return.
- Disconnect the VM's virtual NIC at the hypervisor, or attach it to a proven no-uplink isolated network, for the display run. Guest firewall defaults are insufficient because outbound is currently allowed. This must not alter the physical host's connectivity.
- Confirm in the host VM definition that no host disk/folder other than an explicitly scoped disposable transfer mechanism is attached, USB passthrough and hot-plug are absent, and SPICE file-transfer/shared-folder features are disabled. Guest-side inventory cannot prove those host policies.
- Run the future test from a fresh non-administrator guest account/profile that cannot read `C:\Users\Dev`, with all database/settings/log/document/output paths under a disposable local root. The present Codex sandbox identity is not sufficient because it can read the live Dev profile. Creating the account or changing ACLs is not authorized now.
- After any approved changes, reverify from inside the guest: no `Z:`/SMB/host share, no default route or external DNS, no USB storage, no readable Dev/production paths, and only the intended disposable assets. Retain the sealed reference and working database rules from the pending preparation task. CRM preparation and launch remain separately gated.

### Simplest reliable next step

Prefer a disposable snapshot/clone of this existing QEMU VM so Rob's Excel-testing VM remains unchanged. Before booting the test clone, configure it with no VirtIO-FS share, no network adapter/uplink, no USB passthrough, no SPICE file transfer, and a test-only non-admin profile; expose only a narrowly scoped disposable local test disk/root.
